- TAASD trial: autism-adapted CBT (BIACA) vs standard CBT vs TAU for anxiety in autistic children; PARS means, response rates 92.4/81.0/11.1% — [doi:10.1001/jamapsychiatry.2019.4160](https://doi.org/10.1001/jamapsychiatry.2019.4160)
- Taijin kyofusho across two generations of Japanese families: adolescents higher TKS than parents — [doi:10.1177/0020764011421099](https://doi.org/10.1177/0020764011421099)
- Taijin kyofusho and social anxiety in Indonesia vs Switzerland: self-construal as mediator — [doi:10.3389/fpsyg.2013.00003](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2013.00003)
- Targeting perceived cost vs probability of negative social outcomes in a single session, n=37 with high public speaking anxiety: cost condition superior, mediated by change in social cost estimates — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2009.11.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2009.11.007)
- Task concentration training vs applied relaxation, each followed by cognitive therapy, n=65: TCT superior at within-test, difference disappears after CT added, TCT-CT superior again at 1-year follow-up (effect sizes reaching ~d=3) and superior throughout on self-focused attention — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2005.08.010](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2005.08.010)
- Technology-assisted interventions for SAD, iCBT g=0.84 passive/0.38 active, VRET g=0.82 passive, CBM null except in-lab — [doi:10.1016/j.janxdis.2016.06.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.janxdis.2016.06.007)
- Temperament/character predictors (n=59/100): high baseline harm avoidance predicted poor outcome across treatments — [doi:10.1016/j.psychres.2006.10.003](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.psychres.2006.10.003)
- The anchor network meta-analysis (101 trials, 13,164 participants): individual CBT −1.19 (−1.56 to −0.81) vs waitlist and −0.56 (−1.00 to −0.11) vs psychological placebo; psychodynamic therapy −0.62 (−0.93 to −0.31); psychological placebo −0.63 (−0.90 to −0.36); SSRIs/SNRIs −0.44 (−0.67 to −0.22) vs pill placebo; individual CBT vs the IPT/mindfulness/supportive class −0.82 (−1.41 to −0.24), a class not superior to waitlist; psychodynamic therapy positioned third-line — [doi:10.1016/s2215-0366(14)70329-3](https://doi.org/10.1016/s2215-0366(14)70329-3)
- The earliest dynamic trial, in 42 patients unsuitable for insight-oriented psychotherapy: dynamically oriented supportive therapy and prolonged exposure both superior to relaxation and to the basal condition (information, self-exposure instructions and medication) alone — [doi:10.1111/j.1600-0447.1984.tb01187.x](https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1600-0447.1984.tb01187.x)
- The manualized supportive-expressive psychodynamic treatment for social phobia: CCRT (Wish / Response from Others / Response of Self), shame and unrealistic demands as specific foci, the helping alliance, directive prescriptions against self-devaluation, encouragement to confront feared situations — [doi:10.1521/bumc.2007.71.1.56](https://doi.org/10.1521/bumc.2007.71.1.56)
- The only psychodynamic trial in social anxiety with a credible placebo comparator (n = 30, 12-session psychodynamic group therapy): superior on LSAS, F(1,28) = 4.84, p = 0.036 — [doi:10.1590/s1516-44462004000200003](https://doi.org/10.1590/s1516-44462004000200003)
- The published correspondence contesting the interpretation of the psychodynamic findings — [doi:10.1176/appi.ajp.2013.13060744](https://doi.org/10.1176/appi.ajp.2013.13060744)
- The published criteria for a valid non-inferiority claim (a-priori justified margin, pre-registered power, non-active control) by which a non-significant superiority result cannot be read as equivalence — [doi:10.1016/j.janxdis.2025.102980](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.janxdis.2025.102980)
- The published step-by-step imagery rescripting procedure — [doi:10.1016/j.cbpra.2011.03.002](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.cbpra.2011.03.002)
- Therapist effect grows with treatment length (5,828 patients, 158 therapists) — [doi:10.1080/10503307.2016.1216625](https://doi.org/10.1080/10503307.2016.1216625)
- Therapist effects in low-intensity care (1,376 patients, 38 therapists): ICC 0-1.3% — [doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0099658](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0099658)
- Therapygenetics GWAS meta-analysis (n=2,724): no SNPs, no heritability, no polygenic overlap — [doi:10.1038/s41398-019-0481-y](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41398-019-0481-y)
- Third-wave mindfulness-based CBT in older adults: anxiety g=0.58 with evident publication bias — [doi:10.1002/gps.4621](https://doi.org/10.1002/gps.4621)
- Three-level therapist/clinic model (26,888 patients, 462 therapists): clinic 8.2% vs therapist 3.2% unadjusted — [doi:10.1037/ccp0000388](https://doi.org/10.1037/ccp0000388)
- Training low-anxious volunteers to hold a negative self-image abolishes the normal benign inferential bias — [doi:10.1016/s0005-7967(03)00057-3](https://doi.org/10.1016/s0005-7967(03)00057-3)
- Trait positive emotionality predicts faster response in the same dosing-trial cohort — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2023.104436](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2023.104436)
- Transdiagnostic iCBT: depression-first treatment leaves substantial residual social anxiety — [doi:10.1016/j.janxdis.2015.08.002](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.janxdis.2015.08.002)
- Transdiagnostic internet CBT for anxiety, 46 minutes total clinician time per person — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2010.05.014](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2010.05.014)
- Transfer of the manual into routine practice (49 therapists cluster-randomized; ~10 hours of training): remission 9% vs 16% (OR 0.75, 0.19–2.93, p = .681), response 33% vs 28% (OR 1.24, 0.49–3.16), 36.35 vs 33.86 sessions with 35.4% exceeding 25, therapist allegiance not different (8.61 ± 1.11 vs 8.17 ± 1.38, p = .108), adherence separating only on the disorder-specific subscale — [doi:10.1186/s12888-017-1257-7](https://doi.org/10.1186/s12888-017-1257-7)
- Translated/adapted iCT-SAD in Japan (n=15): d=3.66, 80% reliable improvement, 60% remission — [doi:10.2196/45136](https://doi.org/10.2196/45136)
- Treatment gap and detection-does-not-predict-adequate-treatment finding in primary care — [doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0206357](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0206357)
- Treatment preference in the MBI-SAD vs CBGT trial: twice as many preferred the mindfulness arm; preference matching affected only perceived credibility — [doi:10.1002/cpp.2658](https://doi.org/10.1002/cpp.2658)
- Treatment-specific competence, not adherence, predicts outcome — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2012.09.001](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2012.09.001)
- Trial flow (1,450 screened → 514 randomized → 495 assigned), completer counts (159/209 CBT, 149/207 psychodynamic), session and duration means, and attachment change (psychodynamic arm moved neither attachment anxiety ES −.12 p = .254 nor avoidance ES −.01 p = .976 vs waitlist, while CBT moved both) — [doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0192802](https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0192802)
- Turkish SIAS/SPS variants: SIAS-6/SPS-6 best CFA fit, comparable sensitivity/specificity to full versions (n=36 SAD + 487 general) — [doi:10.1007/s11126-025-10245-4](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11126-025-10245-4)
- Twin heritability of phobias 0.43-0.63, no shared environment, two liability factors — [doi:10.1017/s0033291710002436](https://doi.org/10.1017/s0033291710002436)
- Twin study: phobia risk tied to parental death not separation; parental loss explains 1.5-5.1% of variance, 7.0-20.5% of sibling aggregation — [doi:10.1001/archpsyc.1992.01820020029004](https://doi.org/10.1001/archpsyc.1992.01820020029004)
- UK-developed internet cognitive therapy for SAD delivered in Hong Kong (n=44): adjusted LSAS difference 55.36 (44.32-66.39), d=2.41; 86% vs 5% remission, 73% vs 9% lost diagnosis — [doi:10.1016/j.invent.2022.100539](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.invent.2022.100539)
- Umbrella review of psychosocial interventions in autism: social skills groups only "suggestive" evidence; 4/128 meta-analyses survive low-bias sensitivity analysis — [doi:10.1038/s41380-022-01670-z](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41380-022-01670-z)
- Umbrella review under updated empirically-supported-treatment criteria: moderate-quality evidence in anxiety disorders, recommendation graded "strong" not "very strong" — [doi:10.1002/wps.21104](https://doi.org/10.1002/wps.21104)
- Undiagnosed ASD in university students (0.7-1.9%), none previously diagnosed — [doi:10.1177/1362361310393363](https://doi.org/10.1177/1362361310393363)
- Unguided vs guided vs on-demand internet self-help (n=81): large within-group effect, no between-group difference — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2010.12.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2010.12.007)
- Untrained observer SAFE ratings of adolescent safety behaviors in live peer-confederate interactions (n=105) — [doi:10.1016/j.beth.2020.07.006](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.beth.2020.07.006)
- Updated ACT meta-analysis (60 RCTs, N=4,234): mean ES 0.42, vs cognitive/behavioural comparators 0.16 ns, deterioration from 0.68, ACT rated "experimental" for social anxiety — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2014.07.018](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2014.07.018)
- Upward experimental induction: increasing appearance-related safety behaviours increases social anxiety and BDD symptoms (N=99) — [doi:10.1037/abn0000387](https://doi.org/10.1037/abn0000387)
- US pooled DSM-5-planning prevalence: lifetime 10.7% (morbid risk 13.0%), 12-month 7.4% — [doi:10.1002/mpr.1359](https://doi.org/10.1002/mpr.1359)
- Video feedback moderated by self–observer discrepancy in diagnosed SAD — [doi:10.1016/j.janxdis.2010.04.007](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.janxdis.2010.04.007)
- Video feedback online vs face-to-face: 92% vs 96% thought they looked less anxious than predicted, symptom effect equivalent by format — [doi:10.1016/j.jad.2023.03.017](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jad.2023.03.017)
- Videoconference CBT in Japan, 1-year outcomes and cost-effectiveness; LSAS -30.9 at 12 months (SAD n=8) — [doi:10.2196/17157](https://doi.org/10.2196/17157)
- Videoconference imagery rescripting, multiple-baseline open trial (N=35): pre-post SIAS-6 d=0.81 (0.32–1.29) and SPS-6 d=0.80 (0.30–1.27); 66% remission at post, 74% at 3 months — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2025.104914](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2025.104914)
- VR applications for anxiety disorders, overall ns vs non-VR, g=1.29 vs passive controls — [doi:10.1016/j.jbtep.2023.101893](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jbtep.2023.101893)
- VR exposure vs in-vivo exposure within individual CBT for SAD, three-arm RCT, VR superior at post — [doi:10.1192/bjp.bp.116.184234](https://doi.org/10.1192/bjp.bp.116.184234)
- VR therapy for SAD meta-analysis, vs waitlist and vs CBT, superiority concentrated in small/short trials — [doi:10.1016/j.jad.2023.04.043](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jad.2023.04.043)
- VR vs in-vivo exposure with equivalent exposure dose, social phobia subset favours in vivo — [doi:10.3389/fpsyg.2019.01758](https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2019.01758)
- VRET across anxiety disorders, meta-analysis, vs waitlist/placebo/in vivo, sample-size effect on ES — [doi:10.1016/j.janxdis.2018.08.003](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.janxdis.2018.08.003)
- VRET for SAD specifically, meta-analysis, post/6mo/12mo effect sizes, inferior to in vivo at follow-up — [doi:10.1017/s0033291720003785](https://doi.org/10.1017/s0033291720003785)
- Waitlist as a nocebo condition in psychotherapy trials, and the finding's failure to survive publication-bias adjustment — [doi:10.1111/acps.12275](https://doi.org/10.1111/acps.12275)
- Waitlist controls overestimate psychotherapy effects (333 RCTs, 41,480 participants): waitlist-controlled g = 0.95 (0.85–1.04) vs care-as-usual-controlled g = 0.63 (0.55–0.71), p < 0.001, with pre-post change within waitlist g = 0.37 vs g = 0.64 within care as usual — [doi:10.1017/s2045796024000611](https://doi.org/10.1017/s2045796024000611)
- Waitlist-plus-blinded-rater three-arm SAD trial design (gaze-contingent music reward therapy vs SSRI vs waitlist) — [doi:10.1176/appi.ajp.20220533](https://doi.org/10.1176/appi.ajp.20220533)
- Web-based single-session growth-mindset intervention for adolescent anxiety, no arm separated from active control — [doi:10.2196/63500](https://doi.org/10.2196/63500)
- Weekly trajectories and Granger-causality in CBGT vs MBSR: crossed mechanism pattern, disputing anxious thoughts predicted subsequent change in MBSR — [doi:10.1016/j.brat.2017.06.001](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.brat.2017.06.001)
- WFSBP primary-care short guideline: states combination is a clinically desired strategy, a position the SAD-specific trials do not support — [doi:10.3109/13651501.2012.667114](https://doi.org/10.3109/13651501.2012.667114)
- WFSBP Version 3 Part I guideline (33 experts, 22 countries, 1,007 RCTs, psychotherapies graded by drug-standard methods): SSRIs/SNRIs first-line medication, CBT first-line psychotherapy — [doi:10.1080/15622975.2022.2086295](https://doi.org/10.1080/15622975.2022.2086295)
- WHO low-intensity interventions Problem Management Plus and Step-by-Step, meta-analysis — [doi:10.1002/wps.21129](https://doi.org/10.1002/wps.21129)
- WMH cross-national epidemiology: pooled prevalence, income strata, persistence ratios, days out of role, treatment rates, CIDI validity, no offending-others stem — [doi:10.1186/s12916-017-0889-2](https://doi.org/10.1186/s12916-017-0889-2)
- Working alliance in EFT for SAD (12 patients, 1,008 coded segments): symptoms predicted alliance, alliance did not predict symptoms; rupture–resolution in over half — [doi:10.1097/nmd.0000000000000814](https://doi.org/10.1097/nmd.0000000000000814)
- Working alliance stronger in individual than group SAD therapy but generally unrelated to outcome — [doi:10.1016/j.psychres.2014.07.004](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.psychres.2014.07.004)
- Yo Puedo Sentirme Bien — [doi:10.1186/s13063-022-06255-3](https://doi.org/10.1186/s13063-022-06255-3)
